ParlaMint-AT_2007-07-05-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00028_d7e3017	(Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) In the course of the coalition negotiations, I have done much to make it possible for us to offer more options and more flexibility in child care. I am very glad that this draft is now under consideration, because I am concerned that we are giving families the opportunity to decide for themselves how long any parent wants to stay at home, how long they need time for their child and how they want to reconcile it with work and family. For me, therefore, it is a very positive and welcome measure that we are reforming the child's money and that we can thus make it possible for you, if you get out of the profession shorter, to have a higher child's allowance – almost double the amount of child's money! - can get. This is a very important measure, which mainly helps single-parents because they could not stay at home for 30 months in the past because they could not receive the family with the childcare allowance. They have returned to the profession and lost the child's money. – There will be no such thing in the future: You will have 800 € per month of child benefit. I also hope that fathers will also take advantage of the opportunity and devote themselves more to the care of and relationship with their little children. At €800 we want to give an incentive, so that fathers can also be financially dedicated to the care of their children for a few months, as has been said. So I think this is a very positive measure, and it was high time we reform the childcare money.
ParlaMint-AT_2007-07-05-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00028_d7e3043	Additional question? Please, ladies and gentlemen.

I believe that this would be very positive, because that would be in line with labour law, but also because it would take place on the second birthday of the child. This is often such a moment when you say: Yes, now we want to return to the profession. – We hope that – this is an important point – we will also have sufficient childcare facilities, of which the families know that their children are well removed there. I think this proposal to allow even more flexibility here, for a very sensible and a very good one. I support all the measures that lead to women in particular having a chance to return to work faster. Because we know that: The longer women are out of work, the more difficult it is to get back in. This was the problem with “children’s money old”: it actually resulted in women being pushed out of work. 97 percent of women said they had difficulty finding their way back into the profession, and in reality only every second woman in Austria managed to return to work. That's far too little! We need to increase women's opportunities here, and I am therefore very much in favour of any form of flexibility and support the social partners with regard to their additional proposals.

ParlaMint-AT_2007-07-05-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00028_d7e14234	Mrs. Dr. Kdolsky has now been reported next word. The speaking time should not exceed 20 minutes. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2007-07-05-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00028_d7e14256	Madam President. Honourable Members! Estimated visitors to the gallery! With 5. In the EU, January 2007, the new Regulation 1/2005 on the protection of animals during transport has become valid. With the validity of this Regulation, the directives establishing the basis for the local animal transport legislation on roads, air and railways have been repealed. This federal law on animal transport rules, which is now being adopted, is aimed at the establishment of the national framework for the implementation of the European Regulation and, on the other hand, at promoting the following objectives – and this is very important to me: Firstly, the promotion of domestic food quality. Animal welfare standards have become a central quality criterion for domestic food production. With the consistently ambitious restriction of transport times for intra-Austrian slaughter animal transports to 4.5 hours and their extension only under certain conditions, the Federal Government in Europe sets a clear signal for the protection of transported animals. Animal welfare is more than just a marketing factor in Austrian food production. As evidenced many times, short transport routes and measures that reduce animal stress during loading and unloading as well as during transport also lead to better meat quality and strengthen the Austrian food quality. Secondly, the promotion of a respectful relationship with the animal. Due to the appropriate training measures of those persons who are responsible for the animals during loading or transport and during unloading at the destination, and due to future guidelines on care and care, on hygiene and the manner of transport adapted to the welfare of the animals, a respectful handling of the transported animals is required. Thirdly, the demand and promotion of disease prevention. The spread of disease trains in Europe over the last ten years has always been due to uncontrolled and non-traceable animal transport. Even if it is due to the European animal health legislation is largely excluded from the fact that animals are introduced into European animal transport from an area where there is a disease, it is appropriate to ensure the traceability of animal movements by reporting, documentation and accounting as well as by labelling. With further specifications on the characteristics of vehicles, their cleaning and disinfection, if necessary, the disease prevention is carried to preserve the high Austrian animal health status calculation. What does this law mean for Austria? – Small structures in remote areas mean small numbers of livestock on the farms in Europe. It follows that it is often necessary to ensure the transport of individual animals. Those requirements that are met for the performance of animal transport by the owner or in the context of the neighbourhood aid shall be designed in such a way that there is no disadvantage for small businesses. It should also be taken into account when standardising the requirements – and I think that is an essential element – that transports from remote, geographically unfavourable situations remain a reasonable relationship to long-distance transport in intra-Community trade. Participation in quality seal programmes or the elucidation of the biological economy means ensuring the transfer of individual contracting partners along the food chain. Therefore, the participation of animal owners in certification programs in the quality segment is permitted, for example, as an argument why the immediate nearest slaughterhouse cannot be approached. Only in this way can the consumership's desire for regional, high-quality products be guaranteed and fulfilled in the future. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] Whereas, at European level, a restriction of transport to eight hours and an extension to twelve hours is possible even if there is no extensive equipment available for the trucks, and in the case of long-distance transport within other Member States or in the IGH transport periods up to 24 hours for pigs and 14 hours for bovine animals without breaks, the Austrian law provides that within Austrian slaughter animals are to be transported in principle 4.5 hours and only subject to certain conditions an extension to a maximum of 8.5 hours is possible. In the case of farmed animals and breeding animals, transports which exceed eight hours shall be permitted up to a maximum of ten hours. In order to ensure the uniform interpretation of animal transport standards and the uniform implementation of controls, manuals and checklists are prepared on behalf of my department and made available to all control bodies as from the end of the year. I think that, in the end, we have taken a major further step through constructive cooperation here, and I also believe that within the European Union, we will show that Austria is in line with its position and that it will continue to put the animal welfare measures in the foreground. I would like to thank all of you for your constructive cooperation in the Committee, in the discussions and try to agree to this milestone in Austrian animal welfare legislation today. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-AT_2007-07-05-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00028_d7e15552	Next item is the report by Mrs Franz. Desirable time: 2 minutes. Please, ladies and gentlemen.
ParlaMint-AT_2007-07-05-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00028_d7e15573	Madam President. Mrs. Minister! Estimated Ladies and gentlemen in the House! I, too, am very happy that we in Austria have managed to reduce the duration of the shipments of slaughter and farm animals. We draw a mark across Europe and create one of the most modern, one of the strictest animal transport laws, thus removing ourselves from the rest of the EU countries. Another positive step towards protecting our animals is being taken. The four-and-a-half hours that animals may be transported in a piece are reasonable to the animals. It is certainly very difficult to find a way to protect the transported animals, but also the economic conditions of agriculture. The derogation to authorise up to eight and a half hours of transportation takes account of the economic and also regional circumstances of our agriculture, because it is important to remember that in Austria we have farms which are not connected to the transport areas, lie in mountain villages and thus have longer routes from the outset; we do not want to disadvantage the miners. As I have already mentioned: To find a balance between animal welfare and the economy is the task of politics. The Thayatal Railway was interrupted by the Iron Curtain – and the cross-border traffic between Fratres in Austria and Slavonice in the Czech Republic has not yet been resumed. This means that there have been efforts for more than 16 years to set up a section of 5.4 kilometres, from Waldkirchen to the Czech border. The cost of building this part would have amounted to €7 million in 2004/2005, which would have been supported by the EU. But: Despite Confessions of all political parties and mandates with regard to supporting the restoration of this railway line have not succeeded, but unfortunately God has only remained with declarations of intent. Agreements between the State of Lower Austria, the ÖBB and the Federal Government have not yet been implemented. Unfortunately, this project has become expensive due to flood damage, at about €27 million. Given the importance of this project and the recovery of the whole region, which would bring about the realization of this project, this is almost minor. And it is really not all right not to use this interregional rail axis between Lower Austria and the southern Bohemian region. ==References====External links== A unanimous resolution on this matter was adopted on June 2007 – and I should now like to make the following request: [[Quoted print version removed]] This date is very important because in spring 2009 the first Austrian cross-border exhibition between Telc, Horn and Raabs will be held. So far, we know only the statements of ÖBB CEO Martin Huber, who meant that there would be no reactivation of the Thayatal Railway on this occasion, but that there would only be a bus traffic. That's why I'm asking: If you are already looking at such big projects, forget about them.
